=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return counter
            return False
        
        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res

Observe the following faulty python code. It contains one or more bugs.
Fix the code and output only the fixed code.

```python
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return counter
            return False
        
        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res
```

- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return counter
            return False
        
        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res

I recall a solution:
```
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        forbiddenSet = set(forbidden)
        left = 0
        ans = 0
        for right in range(len(word)):
            for i in range(right, max(right-10, left-1), -1):
                if word[i:right+1] in forbiddenSet:
                    left = i + 1
                    break
            ans = max(ans, right-left+1)
        return ans
```

```
def isForbidden(i, j):
    t = trie
    for k in range(i, j):
        c = word[k]
        if c not in t:
            return False
        t = t[c]
        if "end" in t:
            return k - i + 1
    return False
```

```
...
```

Now, let's think about memory and code style. The final code should be syntactically correct. We can include `from typing import List`. The class method should have colon. The inner function should be defined before use. We'll output:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def isForbidden(i: int, j: int):
            t = trie
            for k in range(i, j):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return k - i + 1
            return False

        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(i, j)
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res
```

I recall a known solution for LeetCode 2781:
```
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            node = trie
            for ch in f:
                if ch not in node:
                    node[ch] = {}
                node = node[ch]
            node['#'] = True
        
        def find(s):
            node = trie
            for i, ch in enumerate(s):
                if ch not in node:
                    return False
                node = node[ch]
                if '#' in node:
                    return i + 1
            return False
        
        ans = 0
        r = len(word)
        for l in range(len(word)-1, -1, -1):
            length = find(word[l:r])
            if length:
                r = l + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, r-l)
        return ans
```

Now, let's think about if the algorithm with index-based `isForbidden` could be further optimized by checking only up to `min(j, i + max_len)` where max_len is max forbidden length. But not necessary. However, if forbidden length unbounded, the traversal could be O(n^2). We can compute max_len and limit range to `min(j, i + max_len)` because no forbidden word longer than max_len can be contained. This would make O(n * max_len) even if forbidden length large? Actually if max_len is large, still O(n*max_len). But if max_len is n, O(n^2). But we can bound by max_len. Should we add this? It doesn't hurt. But if max_len is n, still O(n^2) in worst case. But constraints max_len <=10, so not needed. But adding max_len could improve and is correct. Let's consider.

If we know the maximum length of any forbidden word is `max_len`, then any forbidden substring starting at i has length <= max_len. Therefore, when checking for forbidden prefix starting at i, we only need to inspect up to `min(j, i + max_len)` characters. If no forbidden found within that prefix, no forbidden starting at i can be contained in word[i:j] because any longer forbidden word would exceed max_len. This is true. We can implement:
```
max_len = max(len(f) for f in forbidden) if forbidden else 0
...
def isForbidden(i, j):
    t = trie
    limit = min(j, i + max_len)
    for k in range(i, limit):
        ...
    return False
```
This bounds traversal by max_len. But if max_len is 0 (no forbidden), limit = i, returns False. Good. This is a nice optimization. But is it necessary? It also avoids traversing a long trie path if forbidden words are long. It doesn't change correctness. We can include it. However, if forbidden list is empty, `max_len = 0`, `limit = i`, range empty, returns False. Good.

Now, let's think about if the final code should be exactly:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def isForbidden(i: int, j: int):
            t = trie
            limit = min(j, i + max_len)
            for k in range(i, limit):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return k - i + 1
            return False

        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(i, j)
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res
```

This is a good fixed code.

But let's double-check the max_len optimization with a tricky case: forbidden list contains a word of length 10, but a shorter word of length 5 is also forbidden. max_len=10.
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def isForbidden(i: int, j: int):
            t = trie
            limit = min(j, i + max_len)
            for k in range(i, limit):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return k - i + 1
            return False

        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(i, j)
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res
```

i=2 word[2:4]="cd" false res=max(2,2)=2.
i=1 word[1:4]="bcd" returns2 (bc), j=2, res=max(2,1)=2.
i=0 word[0:2]="ab" false res=max(2,2)=2. Output2. correct.

Now, let's think about a case where a forbidden word is a substring that starts at i but is not a prefix of word[i:j] because j was reduced by a later forbidden word. Example word="xabcy", forbidden=["abc","bc"]. At i=1, "abc" returns3, j=3. At i=0, word[0:3]="xab" no forbidden, res=3. Valid substrings: "xab" length3? x a b no abc/bc, yes. "xabc" contains abc/bc invalid. Max3. Algorithm output3. Good.

What about forbidden=["xab","abc"], word="xabc". At i=1, "abc" returns3, j=3. At i=0, word[0:3]="xab" returns3? forbidden "xab" length3, j=0+3-1=2, res=max(?,2). Valid substrings not containing "xab" or "abc": "x","a","b","c","xa","ab","bc" length2? "bc" valid, "xabc" invalid, "xab" invalid, "abc" invalid. Max2. Algorithm:
n=4, j=4.
i=3 "c" res1.
i=2 "bc" false res2.
i=1 "abc" returns3, j=3, res=max(2,2)=2.
i=0 word[0:3]="xab" returns3, j=2, res=max(2,2)=2. Output2. correct.

What about forbidden=["xab","bc"], word="xabc". Valid substrings: "x","a","b","c","xa","ab","xabc" invalid, "xab" invalid, "abc" contains bc invalid, "bc" invalid. Max2 ("xa","ab"). Algorithm:
i=3 "c" res1.
i=2 "bc" returns2, j=3, res1.
i=1 word[1:3]="ab" false res=max(1,2)=2.
i=0 word[0:3]="xab" returns3, j=2, res=max(2,2)=2. Output2. correct.

Now, let's think about a case where a forbidden word is a substring that starts at i but is not a prefix of word[i:j] because j was reduced by a later forbidden word. Example word="xabcy", forbidden=["abc","bc"]. At i=1, "abc" returns3, j=3. At i=0, word[0:3]="xab" no forbidden, res=3. Valid substrings: "xab" length3? x a b no abc/bc, yes. === RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0

        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def isForbidden(i: int, j: int):
            t = trie
            limit = min(j, i + max_len)
            for k in range(i, limit):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return k - i + 1
            return False

        res = 0
        j = len(word)

        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(i, j)
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)

        return res
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0

        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def isForbidden(i: int, j: int):
            t = trie
            limit = min(j, i + max_len)
            for k in range(i, limit):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return k - i + 1
            return False

        res = 0
        j = len(word)

        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(i, j)
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)

        return res
